國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額為 7,450 億美元 ， 月增0.8%、 年增6.3%；
扣除汽車及零件銷售後月增 0.9%，進一步扣除汽車、零件及加油站後
之核心零售銷售月增 0.2%。加油站、餐飲及非實體零售銷售持續成長，
顯示美國消費需求仍具韌性，惟成長動能較前月略有放緩。 
⚫ 2026 財年美國聯邦政府財政赤字達 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，年增 12.2%，
主因政府支出成長高於收入。9 月單月由去年同期盈餘轉為 269 億美元
赤字，關稅退稅增加壓縮收入，而全年淨利息支出突破1 兆美元、年增
8.4%。在聯邦債務總額逾 40 兆美元下，財政赤字擴大及債務融資成本
攀升，持續考驗財政永續性。 
⚫ 9 月美國成屋銷售年率 399 萬戶，月增 1.5%、年減 2.2%；成屋中位數
售價 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，連續 39 個月上漲。待售庫存增至 160
萬戶 、 供給量4.8 個月 ， 顯示供給改善 ， 惟高利率環境仍抑制購屋需求 。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者物價指數 (CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於 8 月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率由 2.4%
升至 2.6%。受能源價格上揚影響，能源指數月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格
大幅上漲，成為推升通膨之主要因素；住房指數續增0.4%。整體而言，
美國通膨壓力較前月升溫，核心物價漲勢亦略有增強。 
⚫ 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 1.3%，年增率由 5.6%升至 6.3%；能
源價格大漲 10.8%為主要推升因素 ， 帶動商品價格月增2.6%。 服務價格
亦月增 0.8%，核心 PPI 年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%，顯示生產端通膨壓力
持續升高。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月中旬，多位聯準會官員表示通膨仍具壓力。Mary Daly 認為未來可
能仍需升息，但 10 月底會議無迫切行動必要；Beth Hammack 警告若通
膨預期惡化，可能加大緊縮力道；主席 Kevin Warsh 則指出美國經濟仍
具韌性，但能源價格上漲及 AI 投資擴張恐推升通膨壓力。整體而言，
聯準會立場偏鷹，但短期升息時點仍視通膨走勢而定。 
⚫ 美墨關稅談判原接近達成初步協議 ， 惟美國商務部長反對調降汽車及鋼
鋁關稅，使協商進展受阻。墨西哥則爭取降低汽車關稅及擴大零組件抵

⚫ 9 月美國零售銷售持續成長，顯示民間消費仍具韌性，房市交易亦略有

工業生產走疲，前景有待觀察

重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐盟領袖峰會歐盟 27 個成員國普遍支持建立貿易的反制機制。該機制
將賦予歐盟在極端情況下 ， 迅速且全面封鎖特定國家企業進入歐洲單一
市場的權限。

每年 35 萬輛的配額上限(每年可遞增 6%)。 
短評 
⚫ 雖德國財政擴張有助支持工業生產，但法國政治、選舉等不利前景重挫
市場信心，恐會影響企業以及政府在各項支出、投資決策，進而形成拖
累因素。 
⚫ 歐盟的 「下一代歐盟復甦計畫」(NextGenerationEU)將在年底前完成所有
撥款，後續須關注是否有其他替代財政工具來緩解財政真空 (近期歐盟
領袖峰會雖有協商相關議題但無法取得共識)，以繼續支撐景氣動能。

短評

分服務業，顯示企業資本支出需求仍具韌性，第三季設備投資可望恢復
成長。

重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐中貿易談判公布相關細節 ， 未來中國出口至歐盟的混合動力汽車設定
每年 35 萬輛的配額上限(每年可遞增 6%)。依照歐盟推算，中國對歐盟
的混合動力車出口量將較原先潛在規模減少一半以上。 
短評 
⚫ 雖歐中貿易協議不利於汽車出口 ， 但有助於避免歐盟進一步擴大貿易保

等方式因應配額限制，藉此維持在歐洲市場的競爭力與市占率。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.19 
一、 美國—內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
2026 年 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,450 億美元，較
上月增加 0.8%，年增 6.3%。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.9%；進一步
扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增0.2%，顯示整體消費需求仍維持
成長。從主要業別表現觀察，加油站、非實體零售商及餐飲服務與飲酒場所銷售
額均較上月增加，其中加油站及餐飲消費表現相對亮眼，電子及家電商店銷售亦
呈成長。整體而言，9 月美國零售銷售延續擴張走勢，顯示民間消費仍具支撐力
道，且多數主要消費類別維持正成長，惟核心零售增幅較前月放緩，後續仍須關
注物價、就業及金融情勢變化對消費支出的影響。 
2026 年 9 月(2026 財年第 12 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字為 269.0 億美元 ， 較
去年同期 1,979.4 億美元財政盈餘轉為赤字。當月政府收入 5,578.8 億美元，年增
2.6%；政府支出 5,847.8 億美元，年增 69.1%，支出增幅明顯高於收入。收入方
面，9 月關稅淨收入由 8 月的 128.4 億美元降至 59.1 億美元，累計2026 財年關稅
淨收入約 1,732.2 億美元，年減11.1%，關稅退稅增加已部分抵銷關稅收入成長。
支出方面，9 月聯邦政府淨利息支出約 347.9 億美元，全年累計達1 兆 517.5 億美
元 ， 年增8.4%， 反映高利率環境下債務利息負擔持續攀升 。 累計2026 財年全年 ，
美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，較 2025 財年擴大 12.2%。其中，
政府收入年增 3.2%至 5 兆 4,033.3 億美元 ， 支出年增5.5%至 7 兆 3,958.0 億美元 ，
顯示支出成長仍快於收入增加。從支出結構觀察，社會安全、醫療保險、國防及
淨利息支出均較上年增加，持續推升財政壓力。整體而言，美國財政赤字擴大主
要反映政府支出成長強勁、利息負擔居高不下，以及關稅退稅增加削弱部分收入
效果 。 在聯邦債務總額突破40 兆美元背景下 ， 未來財政赤字走勢 、 債務融資成本
及財政永續性問題，仍將是市場關注的重要焦點。 
2026 年 9 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率升至 399 萬戶 ， 較上月增加1.5%，
惟較去年同期減少 2.2%，整體房市成交動能仍偏疲弱。區域表現方面，南部及西
部分別月增 2.8%，中西部持平，東北部月減 2.1%，四大地區銷售量均較去年同
期下滑。房價方面，成屋售價中位數為 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，已連續 39 個

月維持年增走勢。供給端則持續增加，待售成屋庫存升至 160 萬戶，較上月增加
1.9%， 較去年同期增加4.6%， 相當於4.8 個月供給量 ， 高於去年同期的4.5 個月 。
全美住房可負擔指數由去年同期 106.7 降至 105.4， 顯示購屋負擔仍受房價及利率
影響。整體而言，9 月美國成屋市場呈現成交量略有回升、房價維持溫和漲勢及
待售庫存持續累積的格局，顯示供給條件改善，但高利率環境仍對購屋需求形成
壓力。 
2026 年 9 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於上月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；扣除食品與能源之核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，與上月
持平，年增率則由 2.4%升至 2.6%。在細項方面，能源價格持續上揚，能源指數
月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格大幅上漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅逾半，為本月通
膨升溫主因；住房指數月增 0.4%，住房項目價格漲幅尚未明顯趨緩。食品指數則
持平，其中外食價格月增0.2%，家庭食品價格月減 0.1%。此外，二手車及卡車、
機票、休閒娛樂及服飾等價格上漲，通訊價格則下跌。整體而言，9 月美國通膨
明顯回升，主要受汽油價格大幅上漲及住房成本續增帶動；核心 CPI 月增率維持
0.3%，年增率則回升至 2.6%，顯示核心物價壓力較前月略有增強，未來通膨走勢
仍須持續關注能源價格變化及住房等服務項目價格表現。 
 2026 年 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 1.3%，高於上月之
0.5%，年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 2.6%，主要受
能源價格月增 10.8%帶動，以汽油、柴油及航空燃油價格漲幅較為顯著；食品價
格亦月增 1.2%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.8%，主要反映批發及零售
利潤率、運輸服務價格上升。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求 PPI
月增 0.5%，高於上月之 0.3%，年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%。整體而言，9 月 PPI 升
幅明顯擴大，主要反映能源與服務價格同步上揚，顯示生產端成本壓力仍偏高，
後續仍須關注能源價格及運輸成本變化對通膨走勢之影響。 
10 月中旬，多位聯準會官員針對貨幣政策發表看法。舊金山聯準銀行總裁戴
利(Mary Daly) 表示，雖然通膨仍高於 2%目標，未來可能仍需進一步升息，但目
前無迫切必要在 10 月 27 日至 28 日 FOMC 會議立即採取行動，顯示聯準會傾向
先觀察後續數據表現。克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth Hammack)則警告，若
通膨預期持續惡化，聯準會可能必須採取更積極的緊縮措施，以維持物價穩定。
聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)10 月 16 日則表示，美國經濟受穩健就業市場及 AI

投資熱潮支撐而維持韌性，但能源價格上漲及 AI 投資擴張恐推升通膨壓力，並
未對下次利率決策預作表態 。 整體而言 ， 聯準會官員發言顯示政策立場仍偏鷹派 ，
但對短期升息時點保持彈性，未來通膨及通膨預期變化仍是利率決策的重要依據。 
近期美墨貿易談判再度受到關稅爭議影響。據《華爾街日報》報導，雙方原
已接近達成初步協議 ， 美國擬調降對墨西哥汽車 、 鋼鐵及鋁製品的部分進口關稅 ，
以換取墨西哥在貿易政策、商品採購及其他經貿議題上的讓步，並為後續《美墨
加協定》 （USMCA） 檢討與延長協商創造有利條件 。 然而 ， 美國商務部長Howard 
Lutnick 反對大幅調降目前對墨西哥汽車課徵的 25%關稅 ， 以及鋼鐵 、 鋁製品與部
分衍生製成品的 50%關稅，使談判進展受阻。墨西哥方面則希望將汽車關稅降至
15%或以下，以縮小與日本、南韓汽車適用稅率的差距，並擴大汽車關稅抵減機
制對美墨加三國零組件價值的認列範圍；惟美方仍須考量國內金屬產業對降稅可
能衝擊本土生產及就業的疑慮。儘管雙方對關稅調整幅度仍存分歧，墨西哥經貿
官員表示協商已有部分進展，美國商務部亦強調持續與墨方保持溝通，顯示談判
尚未正式破局。整體而言，美墨關稅協商結果不僅攸關北美汽車及金屬產業供應
鏈成本與競爭力，也可能影響 USMCA 後續檢討與延長協商進程；若雙方遲遲無
法縮小分歧，恐增加企業投資與供應鏈布局的不確定性，並對北美區域貿易整合
形成壓力。 
二、歐元區—工業生產走疲，前景有待觀察 
 歐元區 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)較 7 月下滑 0.5%，呈現連續三個月下降，
其中非耐久消費財、中間財及能源產出分別減少 2.7%、0.8%、0.6%，僅資本財、
耐久消費財產出分別成長 0.4%、0.2%。儘管德國 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)反彈
0.4%，係受惠車廠陸續結束歲修，但法、義、西工業生產分別下跌0.3%、1.3%、
0.8%為主要拖累因素，反映先前美伊戰爭帶來的庫存儲備需求逐漸消退，尤其以
能源密集產業(化工、造紙、 鋼鐵等)變化最為明顯。整體而言，即便排除能源密集
產業的波動，其他製造業諸如製藥、汽車、機械等生產情形皆偏疲軟。 
 展望未來，近期德國在財政擴張的支持下，工廠訂單大幅成長，未來可望陸
續反映在該國工業生產表現；但法國政治、選舉等不利前景重挫市場信心，恐會
影響企業以及政府在各項支出 、 投資決策 ， 進而形成拖累因素 。 另外 ， 歐盟的 「下
一代歐盟復甦計畫」(NextGenerationEU)已在 9 月底截止撥款申請 ， 並將在年底前

三、日本—AI 投資續強，帶動機械訂單穩健復甦 
 8 月核心機械訂單(扣除波動訂單)月增 5.2%，其中製造業訂單月增 1.6%，人
工智慧相關投資仍是主要驅動力，資通訊產業訂單月增 13.0%，有色金屬也大幅
反彈；汽車產業月增 13.1%，並已連續四個月成長，顯示投資需求穩健。非製造
業訂單(扣除波動訂單)月增 2.9%，其中租賃業成長尤為強勁；運輸業、建築業在
7 月大幅成長後出現回落；零售業則在連續三個月下滑後月增 29.2%。 整體而言 ，
機械訂單數據顯示企業資本支出呈現更廣泛且穩固的復甦趨勢，尤以 AI 相關投
資為主要支撐。 
四、中國—AI 相關需求帶動出口動能強勁成長 
9 月出口年增率較 8 月大幅增加 11.3 個百分點至 36.3%，出口總值為 4,472
億美元 ； 累計前九個月出口年增率上升為21.3%， 累積出口總值為33,707 億美元 。
前九個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(108.1%)、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等，54.6%)、船舶(29.9%)、汽車(54.3%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備
出口加速成長，為 9 月出口年增率大幅上升的主要因素。 
9 月進口年增率較 8 月增 2.8 個百分點至 31%，進口總值為 3,137 億美元；累
計前九個月進口值成長 27.5%至 24,326 億美元。其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等)進口分別成長 63.5%、124.8%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部
分伺服器關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前九個月貿易順差達
9,381 億美元，僅較去年同期增加 7.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時
伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價
值產品依賴國際供應鏈支持。 
9 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 8 月的 3.8%升至 5%， 主因原油價格自7 月起走
升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年增率
續揚 14.1 個百分點至 24.5%，並帶動燃料加工業出廠價格年增率上揚至 21.6%，
此外，對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學品、化纖出廠價格年增率加速升至
12.1%、12.3%，顯示原油價格上漲的影響開始擴散至中下游產業。此外，由於近
期鋼鐵價格走弱 ， 黑色金屬礦產開採 、 冶煉加工業出廠價格分別年減3.4%、0.2%，

9 月消費者通膨(CPI)升 0.1 個百分點為 0.9%，但仍然是今年以來第三低的水
準，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)持平於 1 %不變。該月燃油通膨續增 6.6 個百分點
至 14.9%，在客運成本增加之下，旅遊服務通膨亦增加 0.8 個百分點至 3.5%，此
外，由於手機價格上漲，通信工具通膨上揚 3.7 個百分點至 14.3%；其他商品及
服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，雖然企業生產成本持續上升，但在民間消費乏
力之下，企業仍大多自行吸收成本，加上政府壓抑零售燃油及民生水電，故目前
原油價格高漲的影響尚未反映於消費端價格。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，
若油價維持高檔或進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。 
展望未來，歐中談判已就汽車貿易達成部分共識，包含未來中國出口至歐盟
的混合動力汽車設定每年 35 萬輛的配額上限，且配額規模可逐年增加 6%。依照
歐盟預估 2026 年自中國進口約 80 萬輛混合動力汽車推算，中國對歐盟的混合動
力車出口量將較原先潛在規模減少一半以上。雖該協議不利於汽車出口，但有助
於避免歐盟進一步擴大貿易保護措施，中國車廠則可能會加速透過赴歐投資設
廠、提升當地生產比重等方式因應配額限制，藉此維持在歐洲市場的競爭力與市
占率。

下行風險，建議各國政府提升農業生產效率及糧食安全，提升數位經濟及服務業
競爭力，強化財政紀律並提高公共投資效率，維持穩健貨幣政策與匯率穩定。

六、台灣—AI 帶動出口、投資與消費成長，中經院上修 2026 年經濟預測 
中經院 10 月 16 日公布最新預測，上修 2026 年台灣經濟成長率至 12.16%，
創 1988 年以來新高，主要受惠於 AI 技術快速發展帶動全球科技投資，半導體與
資通訊產品需求強勁，出口維持高速成長，同時民間投資與消費亦同步擴張，經
濟成長動能由內、外需共同支撐。另一方面，由於中東地緣政治風險升溫帶動油
價維持高檔，能源價格外溢效果推升整體物價，中經院將 2026 年 CPI 年增率預
估上修至 2.06%，惟整體通膨仍屬相對溫和。

客戶缺料及平台轉換影響拉貨，4Q26 EPS 預估季減 22% 
川湖 3Q26 營收 173.38 億元，季增 60.1%、年增 296.9%，低於預期 7.3%。
7–8 月稼動率 80%，9 月降至 60%，主因客戶記憶體缺料、新舊平台轉換及
新創客戶一次性建置完成，因此維持 3Q26 毛利率預估 87.2%，EPS 下修
7.2%至 119.52 元。4Q26 營收則以 9 月作為基準，客戶拉貨取決於急缺零
組件到位情況，備料完成隨時啟動拉貨。預估 4Q26 營收 135.61 億元，季
減 21.8%，毛利率 86.4%，EPS 下修 28.2%至 92.91 元，季減 22.3%，反
映客戶備料及平台轉換影響。 
1H27 VR 量產與周邊機櫃導入，台美擴產支應成長 
1H27 進入 VR 量產週期，除運算機櫃外，電源、CPU 等周邊 Side Rack 同
步導入，擴大導軌應用並提高單一系統供貨價值。新平台設計與加工難度提
升，帶動高階導軌量價成長。擴產方面，美國休士頓廠已出貨，川湖二廠仍
在興建，預計 2H27 投產；三、五期目前處於設計階段，預計 2027 年動工。
三期規模相當於現有廠房面積，五期與三期相同，擴建完成後整體產能至少
為現有 3 倍。因規模較大，建廠需 2–2.5 年，預計 2029 年底至 2030 年完
工。本中心預估 2027 年營收 730.53 億元，年增 54.8%，EPS 500.57 元。 
維持川湖買進評等，目標價 15,000 元 
考量記憶體缺料、新舊平台轉換及新創客戶一次性建置完成， 本次下修
2026／2027 年營收預估 12.3%／13.7%至 471.78／730.53 億元，年增
169.6%／54.8%；EPS 下修 12.4%／14.0%至 323.39／500.57 元，年增
213.3%／54.8%。看好川湖導軌製造能力， 1H27 VR 量產及周邊機櫃導入
帶動需求，二廠 2H27 投產增加產能，三、五期支應中長期成長。維持
2027 年 30 倍本益比，以 EPS 500.57 元推得目標價 15,000 元，維持買進。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
記憶體缺料、平台轉換及新創客戶一次性建置完成，影響短期拉貨，
下修 3Q26／4Q26 EPS 7%／28%至 119.52／92.91 元。 
⧫  
1H27 VR 量產與周邊機櫃導入帶動高階導軌需求，川湖二廠 2H27 投
產增加供應能力，預估 2027 營收 730.53 億元，年增 54.8%。 
⧫  
下修 2026／2027 年 EPS 預估 12%／14%至 323.39 元／500.57 元，
維持 2027 年 30 倍本益比，目標價調整至 15,000 元，維持買進。

營運分析
3Q26 營收低於預期 7%，EPS 預估下修至 119.52 元
川湖 3Q26 營收 173.38 億元，季增 60.1%、年增 296.9%，低於本中心預估 7.3%。7–8 月稼動率
約 80%，9 月降至 60%，主要受到 1）客戶記憶體缺料，影響整機備料及組裝進度；2）新舊平台
轉換；3）部分 AI 新創客戶一次性建置完成等因素影響，導致 9 月營收月減約 30%。本中心維持
3Q26 毛利率預估 87.2%，季減 0.2 個百分點；營業利益預估下修 7.3%至 141.79 億元，季增
59.5%、年增 376.6%；稅後淨利預估 113.90 億元，季增 60.7%、年增 256.3%，EPS 預估下修
7.2%至 119.52 元。

客戶備料進度影響拉貨節奏，4Q26 營收預估季減 22%
公司表示，客戶拉貨節奏相當不規律，主要取決於急缺零組件取得情況；當記憶體等材料到位、整
機具備組裝條件後，即可啟動拉貨，因此單月出貨仍將隨客戶備料進度波動。目前客戶專案需求預
測未見明顯異常，但缺料與新舊平台轉換使需求釋出時點較難平均分布。
本中心下修 4Q26 營收預估 27.9%至 135.61 億元，季減 21.8%、年增 174.0%；考量出貨規模及
產能利用率調整，毛利率預估下修 0.8 個百分點至 86.4%，季減 0.8 個百分點。營業利益預估
109.79 億元，季減 22.6%、年增 220.6%；稅後淨利預估 88.54 億元，季減 22.3%、年增 152.0%，

EPS 預估下修 22.3%至 92.91 元。

1H27 進入 VR 量產週期，周邊 Side Rack 擴大導軌需求
1H27 進入 Vera Rubin 量產週期，除運算機櫃外，電源、CPU 等周邊 Side Rack 亦須配合整體系
統導入，導軌需求由運算設備延伸至周邊基礎設施。成長來源除新平台出貨增加，亦包含不同功能
機櫃增加，以及導軌設計與加工難度提高，推升單一系統的供貨價值。

各類導軌依機構設計與加工要求定價，部分電源及 CPU 應用的導軌複雜度高於 GPU 應用，產品價
值不隨設備售價等比例下降。川湖既有產品原則上於生命週期內維持議定價格，新世代產品則依升
級規格重新報價，因此平均單價提升主要來自新產品導入及產品組合優化。本中心看好 VR 量產、
ASIC 平台升級及周邊應用增加，帶動 2027 年高階導軌量價成長，預估全年營收 730.53 億元，年
增 54.8%，毛利率 86.5%，年增 0.6 個百分點，EPS 500.57 元。
美國休士頓廠已於 10 月投產；川湖二廠則預計 2H27 投產
川湖二廠目前仍在興建，預計 2H27 投產；美國休士頓廠則已於 9–10 月向首批客戶出貨，完整產
能開出後將增加整體產能約 20%，目前生產效率約為台灣廠的一半，公司透過人員訓練及製程改
善提高效率，2027 年持續提升產出。部分 AI 專案交期急迫，空運成本甚至高於導軌本身，北美在
地生產可縮短交付時間，支援客戶急單及補貨需求。休士頓廠產出提升與二廠 2H27 投產，將分階

段增加供應能力，配合 VR 及其他新平台的出貨需求。
川益三、五期預計 2027 年動工，2029 年底至 2030 年完工
川益三、五期已開始設計，後續須完成審圖及發包，預計 2027 年動工。三期規模相當於目前所有
廠房面積的加總，五期則與三期規模相同；依公司說明，擴建完成後整體產能至少達現有的 3 倍。
由於工程規模較大，建廠時間約需 2–2.5 年，預計 2029 年底至 2030 年完工，支應後續新世代平
台及周邊應用需求。
公司已通過三、五期廠房新建及設備採購預算 197.12 億元，依工程與設備採購進度分期投入，並
規劃發行面額上限合計 100 億元、年期 5 年、票面利率 0%的可轉債，資金用於轉投資川益科技。
三、五期定位為中長期產能布局，2027 年新增供應主要來自休士頓廠產出提升及二廠投產。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：組裝及零組件產業 2026 年 11 月 4 日 King Slide 第4 頁，共 20頁  
產品結構分析  
預估川湖 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收比重達 84%，多元平台架構支撐長期展望 
川湖具備高承載、高耐用度及客製化滑軌設計能力，AI 伺服器對於滑軌承重、抗震與散熱協同設計
要求提升，形成新進者難以突破的技術門檻。川湖目前 2Q26 產品組合為伺服器 94%、6%，本中
心預估川湖 2026/2027 年 AI 伺服器營收比重分別達 78%/84%，其中以 GB/VR/ASIC 系列為主軸，
並擴產至 NVDIA Groq、Spectrum-X 交換器、AMD MI450 以及 Neo Cloud 等新平台架構。 
展望 2026-2027 年，我們預估川湖於整體伺服器導軌供貨份額達 90%以上，並看好川湖伺服器導
軌成長動能無虞，主因包含 1) GB300 單一機櫃導軌產值估較 GB200 成長 80%，數量由 2.5 萬櫃
提升至 6.8 萬櫃；2) Vera Rubin(VR200) 平台預計 2H26 登場，預計 4Q26 量產，預估 2027 年達
7-8 萬櫃；3) AWS T3 需求強勁，預期 AWS ASIC 晶片量將 2026/2027 年分別年增 25%/70%至
200/340 萬顆；4) 受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發，預估 2026 年通用型伺服器出貨量將年增 23%。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：組裝及零組件產業 2026 年 11 月 4 日 King Slide 第5 頁，共 20頁  
同業比較分析  
川湖為 AI 導軌龍頭廠商，專利護城河將確保公司穩居領先地位 
川湖在歐美、台灣及中國皆有完整的專利佈局，目前專利核准數高達 3,641 件，深厚的專利壁壘使
競爭對手極難繞過，無法輕易生產高可靠度產品。同時，川湖具備高承載、高耐用度及客製化滑軌
設計能力，能完美對應 AI 伺服器對滑軌承重、抗震與散熱協同設計的嚴格要求，形成新進者難以
突破的技術門檻。在市場定位與市佔表現方面，對比國內同業南俊國際（ 6584 TT ）在 GB 及 
ASIC 等 AI 導軌出貨比重仍低，川湖的整體伺服器導軌市佔率預估高達 90% 以上，專利護城河將
確保公司穩居領先地位；就獲利率而言，由於川湖的  AI 導軌營收佔比較高，整體獲利能力亦優於
南俊國際。 
我們持續看好公司憑藉極高的技術門檻與產品可靠度，產品線全面涵蓋次世代  AI 伺服器（如  
GB300、Vera Rubin 平台）、ASIC 伺服器、網路交換器及 Neo Cloud 等多元平台，以因應強勁
需求。此外，川湖的擴廠計畫包含：1）美國德州新廠已於 2026 年 10 月開始出貨；2）台灣二廠
預估於 2H27；3）川益三期、五期預期 2029 年底至 2030 年完工，預期將支撐中長期成長。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：組裝及零組件產業 2026 年 11 月 4 日 King Slide 第7 頁，共 20頁  
財務分析  
川湖整體財務槓桿維持極低且健全的水準，負債比長年保持在  20% 以下，顯著低於同業平均，展
現強健的抗風險能力；流動比率則維持在 5.5 至 8 倍，優於同業平均之表現，顯示公司流動性充裕、
短期償債無虞。在存貨管理方面，存貨周轉天數近年約落在  100 至 150 天區間震盪，略高於同業
平均。受惠於產品組合優化與高毛利訂單放量，ROE 與 ROA 呈現逐年走揚態勢，營運效率持續改
善。整體而言，本中心認為川湖在極佳的資本結構與流動性保護下，獲利能力與資產運用效率同步
提升，財務體質維持高度穩健。

### Page 8

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：組裝及零組件產業 2026 年 11 月 4 日 King Slide 第8 頁，共 20頁  
產業概況  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 96%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic)、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國主要 CSP 資本支出將年增 96%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA GB300 的天下，AWS、Google、
Meta ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將
採購 TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，本中心預估 2026 年通用型伺服器出貨量將年增
23%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP 等業
者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中將消
耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 9

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：組裝及零組件產業 2026 年 11 月 4 日 King Slide 第9 頁，共 20頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，年增
15%及 31%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用 GPU 算力需求仍維持
成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者， 2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下， CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本， ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

獲利調整與股票評價
維持川湖買進評等，目標價 15,000 元
考量客戶記憶體缺料、新舊平台轉換及新創客戶一次性建置完成，本次下修 2026／2027 年營收預
估 12.3%／13.7%至 471.78／730.53 億元，年增 169.6%／54.8%；毛利率分別下修 0.4／0.4 個
百分點至 85.9%／86.5%，EPS 下修 12.4%／14.0%至 323.39／500.57 元，年增 213.3%／54.8%。
本中心看好川湖機構設計、模具開發及自動化製造整合能力，1H27 VR 量產及周邊 Side Rack 導
入帶動高階導軌需求，二廠 2H27 投產增加供應能力，三、五期則支應中長期成長。維持 2027 年
30 倍本益比，以 EPS 500.57 元推得目標價 15,000 元，維持買進評等。

公司簡介  
川湖為 AI 發展浪潮下之主要伺服器導軌受惠廠商  
川湖科技成立於 1986 年，2000-2001 年進入伺服器市場，目前伺服器、網路通訊、雲端資料中心及其週邊設備產品的比重
最高，其次是廚具相關產品，而鉸鏈、滑軌產品主要用於傢俱、鋼櫃、工具櫃、木製傢俱等產品。川湖員工數約 1,500 人，
廠房位於高雄市路竹區(川湖總部)及高雄科學園區(川益科技)。2026 年第二季營收比重中，伺服器業務佔營收約 94%、廚
具與其他業務則約為 6%。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：組裝及零組件產業 2026 年 11 月 4 日 King Slide 第17 頁，共 20頁

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 川湖整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於中等風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於略為領先
的位置，在電子零組件行業的公司中排名略微領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 川湖的整體曝險屬於中等水準，略為落後電子零組件行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  ESG 
議題包含營運方面的碳排、公司在生產中使用原材料的效率(不包括能源和石油產品)、人力資源等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：川湖在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬上等。公司未曾涉入重大財務事件。 
 圖 31：ESG 分析

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

產業速報

1 群益投顧 
台達電 (2308 TT) 公布 3Q26 營收為 1,970.59 億元、 QoQ+7.5% ； 因 提 列 庫 存 跌 價 損 失
影 響，毛 利 率 降 至 34.1% ，營 業 利 益 率 為 16.6% ；稅 後 淨 利 為 234.95 億 元、QoQ -6.5% ，
稅後 EPS 為 9.04 元 。 隨 缺 料 情 況 改 善、 Vera Rubin 產品 量產出貨 ， 預期 4Q26 營運
將有顯著提升 。 受 惠 於 AI 、 Data Center 領 域 需 求 成 長 ， 營 收 與 獲 利 維 持 成 長 趨 勢 ，
投資評等為 Buy 。

研 究 員 ： 王 美 珍   
iris.wang@capital.com.tw

3Q26 稅後 EPS 為 9.0 4 元 ：  
台 達 電 公 布 3Q26 營 收 為 1, 970.59 億 元 、 QoQ+ 7.5%； 因 提 列 庫 存 跌 價 損 失
影響 ， 毛 利 率 降 至 34.1% ， 營 業 利 益 率 為 16.6 %； 稅 後 淨 利 為 234.9 5 億 元 、
QoQ -6.5 %， 稅 後 EPS 為 9. 04 元 ， 低 於 預 期 。  
產 品 規 格 提 升 ， 電源 類 產 品 展 望 佳：  
台達電 為 AI 伺 服 器 電 源 解 決 方 案 主 要 供 應 商， 受 惠 於 AI 需 求 提 升 ， 帶 動
AC/DC 電 源 大 幅 成 長 外 ，CSP 客 戶 在 新 一 代 ASIC 伺 服 器 平 台 採 用 DC/DC
電 源 模 組 ， 亦 成 為 另 一 成 長 動 能 。800 VDC 與 ± 400VDC 預 計 於 4Q26 出貨 ，
2026 年 營 收 比 重 約 1% ，預 期 2H27 營 收 貢 獻 較 顯 著。  
水 冷 散 熱 2026 年 營 收 維 持 成 長 趨 勢：  
水冷散熱產品有水冷板、 Manifold 、 CDU 、 Sidecar 。目前出貨以系統類的
Sidecar 為 主 ， 歸 類 於 基 礎 建 設； CDU 則 歸 類 於 電 源 類 與 零 組 件 。 水 冷 散 熱
2025 年 營 收 比 重 為 9%，台 達 電 Sidecar 產品 2025 年 市 佔 率 極 高，雖 有 競 爭
者 加 入 ， 但 整 體 市 場 需 求 持 續 增 長 ，2026 年 Sidecar 營 收 仍 將 維 持 成 長 趨
勢，預估 2026 年 水 冷 散 熱 營 收 比 重 為 12% 。  
固 態 變 壓 器 為 長 期 成 長 動 能：  
SST (固 態 變 壓器 )目 前 已 少 量 出 貨，客 戶 為 中 系 CSP 、800V 高 壓 直 流 電 動 車
充 電 站，亦 出 貨 給 美 系 客 戶 測 試。SST 導 入 需 從 資 料 中 心 設 計 初 期 即 納 入 規
劃，預期  2~3 年 後 會 產 生 顯 著 的 營 收 貢 獻 ， 仍 持 續 改 良 產 品 規 格 中 。 
投 資 建 議 ：  
隨 缺 料 情 況 改 善、Vera Rubin 產 品 量 產 出 貨，預 期 4Q26 營 運 將 有 顯 著 提 升。
受惠於 AI 、 Data Center 領 域 需 求 成 長 ， 營 收 與 獲 利 維 持 成 長 趨 勢 ， 投 資 評
等為 Buy 。

產業速報

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Industry View In-Line
(-) PC OEMs and ODMs: Intel (covered by Joseph Moore) expects 2H26 PC units to
fall 20%+ y/y as OEMs aggressively cut inventories amid weaker sell-through; the
correction has accelerated over the past two months. This aligns with Morgan

Stanley's estimate at a ~25% y/y decline  (report link).
(+) Server OEMs and ODMs: Intel guides for server CPU unit growth of 30%+ in
both 2026 and 2027, with upside if wafer/substrate supply improves. It sees server
CPU TAM rising from US$40bn today to >US$200bn in five years, driven mainly by
AI agents.
(+) ABF substrates:  Most of the previously discussed ~40% capacity increase is due
in 2H27; Intel expects key component purchase commitments to rise sharply over

the next several quarters. Intel is also prioritizing securing substrate capacity and
improving yields for the EMIB-T. Complex designs add yield risk, which could lower
effective output, worsening ABF substrate shortages but adding further support for
pricing.

Key Takeaways
We view negative PC comments as more bearish for OEMs and ODMs positioned
in the lower end consumer segment, like Acer and Compal.
Strong CPU unit growth would be positive for Wiwynn, Lenovo, and Lotes.
Tighter ABF substrate supply and stronger pricing would be positive for
Unimicron and NYPCB's revenue and margins.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

## Document 176: 261105_gs_nypcb

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_gs_nypcb.pdf

### Page 2

Investment Thesis - NYPCB 
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit 
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB 
considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate 
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the 
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the 
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end 
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding 
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s 
high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook 
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger 
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the 
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long 
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s 
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148% 
2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s 
earnings and stock performance.  
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB 
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based 
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).  
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2) 
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3) 
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.

前評價已回落至 17.7 倍，位於低檔。綜上所述，研究部下修 2027 年
營收至 295 億元 （原估316 億元） ，EPS 下修至 121.74 元 （原估124.48
元），給予 24 倍本益比，目標價下調至 2,920 元。

重點摘要： 
1. 產品組合大幅優化，3Q26毛利率躍升至38.0% 
3Q26營收45億元，季增20.4%，年增26.3%，符合預期。其中：
(1)3Q成長主要來自伺服器QD，季增將近8成，主因GB300持續
出貨，加上VR200小量出貨；(2)中系手機部分，受記憶體漲價
及缺貨影響，終端拉貨力道疲弱，單季營收季減51%；(3)滑軌
部分，因通用伺服器記憶體缺料影響，季減將近4成。整體而
言，因QD營收大幅提升，伺服器營收占比由2Q的55%大幅提升
至3Q的81%，產品組合轉佳，故毛利率達38.0%（原估37.0%），
季增4.0ppts，優於預期。營益率32.4%（原估32.4%） ，季增
4.5ppts，符合預期；EPS達18.11元（原估17.57元） ，優於預
期。

2. 4Q26營收微幅下修，惟毛利率續創新高 
展望4Q26，考量中系手機客戶雖為旺季， 但受缺料及漲價影響 ，
拉貨態度偏保守。在VR部分，根據供應鏈調查，若以L11機櫃
組裝端來看，4Q26~1Q27僅小量出貨，真正放量須至2Q27，時
程遞延。但若以零組件端看，液冷QD屬於偏L6端的產品，出貨
時程較早，4Q已開始出貨。但考量放量時程及手機影響，研究
部微幅下修4Q26營收至51億元 （原估53億元） ，季增12.7%，年
增27.6%。毛利率部分，考量伺服器占比可望維持在高檔，上
修毛利率至38.6%（原估37.3%） ，季增0.6ppts；EPS微幅下修
至20.42元（原估20.89元）。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. NV、ASIC雙引擎並進 
基本面仍維持正向看待，QD成長動能明確：(1)NV VR 4Q逐漸
出貨，動能延續至 27年；下一代Ultra設計將導入更高流量
ZQD(單價翻倍)， 促進價值提升；(2)AWS T3已配合客戶時程出
貨，新世代平台水冷滲透率進一步提升；(3)其他ASIC專案陸
續進入量產，本次新增MSFT Maia QD，預估單櫃約576顆(32顆
*18層)，Google TPU、Meta也陸續出貨。產能方面，QD月產能
已擴充至450萬顆（+50%），2027年持續擴產，預期提升至650
萬顆，足以支撐後續訂單需求。

4. VR放量時程遞延，保守下修2027年營收 
然而 ，研究部仍考量以下因素 ， 保守微幅下修2027年營收預估 ：
(1)NV平台部分，考量VR延後至2Q27放量，恐連帶影響Rubin 
Ultra，且目前機櫃架構亦尚未定案，ZQD用量存在變數；(2)
中系手機客戶整體拉貨動能偏弱， 研究部預估明年衰退，故些
微下修27年預估。

5. 目前評價已回檔，潛在空間浮現，維持買進建議 
綜上所述 ， 研究部下修2026年營收至175億元 （原估178億元） ，
年增41.0%，惟毛利率上修至36.0%（原估35.4%），EPS上修至
64.30元 （原估64.20元）。同步下修2027年營收至295億元 （原
估316億元），EPS微幅下修至121.74元 （原估124.48元） 。目前
評價已回落至17.7倍，位於低檔，潛在空間已浮現，給予24倍
本益比，目標價由3,115元調整至2,920元，維持買進建議。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

## Document 240: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡_62bfa2bd

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY) 을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%) 이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY) 을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 109,000원 
현재주가 (10.29) 56,700원

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation  that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement  to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East  could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement ,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI .
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady  at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy .
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

### Page 8

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

## Document 356: GFHK - Apple F4Q26 review

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - Apple F4Q26 review.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Muted iPhone 18 Pro Cycle and Rising Memory Costs

[Table_Rank]

Maintained  Hold  
 Target price US$334

[Table_Summary] Lower TP to $334, Hold rating: Driven by investors being away from AI themes, Apple share 
price outperformed Nasdaq by 7.2% in the past one month. However, we continue to expect 
muted cycle of iPhone 18 P/PM with constrained volume of iPhone Duo. Additionally, memory 
cost hike will last longer than expected, driven by our view of tight DRAM/NAND supply, and 
we expect cost hikes throughout CY2027. For hardware, we still expect the iPhone 20 (2H27) 
to be a strong product cycle, thanks to new design of display, followed by Apple’s early move 
to TSMC ’s A14 in 2H28 (suggesting only 2 -years at 2nm). Additionally, Apple Intelligence 
remains the key to watch. Yet, the share price trades at 35x FY27E P/E, suggesting the stock 
isn’t cheap. By factoring in results, guidance and lower shipments/margins, we revise FY27E 
EPS by -10% and revise TP from $369 to $334, still based on 35x FY27E. 
Dec-Q margin guidance below expect ations: Apple delivered solid Sep t-Q results, with 
revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of $2.1 (+14% YoY), both ahead of 
consensus. For Dec-Q, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY (vs. consensus 
of +6.5%), Services growth of 8 –9%, and gross margin of 45 –46%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive to Street expectations, though the margin guidance and Services deceleration 
were disappointing, reflecting cost hikes and mix headwinds.  
The iPhone product cycle mixed, with challenges in FY2027: Mgmt sees a strong start of 
iPhone Duo but remains supply constrained. Hence, we continue to expect the EMS build plan 
of 6.5m of iPhone Duo by end-2026 with limited upside, while the mainstream iPhone 18 P/PM 
to be 74.5m. As said in our earlier report of iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max Lukewarm on Sept 15, we 
believe the muted demand will weigh on CY1H27 EMS builds. Margin wise, despite p ricing 
actions taken earlier, margin will likely be under pressure, as the CEO Ternus characterized 
current memory pricing as “unprecedented,” with higher costs in December and beyond.  
Apple positioning the rebuilt Siri AI as a core differentiator: The CEO Ternus emphasized 
that the new Siri is deeply integrated and privacy -preserving and is fielding ~2.5bn requests 
per day. From a financial perspective, CFO Parekh noted that these Si ri AI costs are 
embedded in the December margin outlook, suggesting near-term gross-margin compression 
alongside the memory cost up-cycle.  
Risks: 1) weak demand, 2) competition, 3) product delays, 4) geo-tension.

### Page 2

Earnings Forecast  
Solid September Quarter Results  
Apple delivered a solid September-quarter, with revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of 
$2.10 (+14% YoY), both ahead of consensus, reaching a record fiscal year of $479bn in revenue. Gross 
margin came in at 47.9%, aided by approximately one percentage point of tariff refund benefits (and a 
$0.05 EPS tailwind).  
iPhone revenue reached a September-quarter record of $57.6bn (+17.5% YoY) likely driven by higher 
ASPs, Mac rose a strong 24% to $10.8bn likely on edge-AI, Services set at $31.6bn (+9.8% YoY) which 
was below expectations, while iPad declined 10% to $6.2bn and Wearables fell 8% to $8.3bn. The 
company returned $28bn to shareholders. 
December Quarter Guidance Slightly Ahead in Revenue but Shy in Margin  
For the December quarter, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY ( vs. consensus of 
+6.5%), Services growth of 8–9%, and gross margin of 45–46%. Operating expenses are expected at 
$20.4–20.8bn, OI&E around $300 million, and the tax rate near 17%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive relative to prior Street expectations, though the margin range and Services deceleration 
reflect known cost and mix headwinds.

### Page 5

Valuation and Recommendation  
Hold with TP of $334 
We rate Apple (AAPL US) Hold and set our TP at $334 which is based on 35x our FY27E EPS estimate. 
We believe it is important to capture the 2H26 -3Q27’s iPhone 18 product cycle, given high market 
interest. We adopt P/E methodology as we believe the market now mainly focuses on the company’s 
earnings outlook, and we usually apply P/E method to downstream firms.  
We believe Apple’s ecosystem will remain strong and associated revenue durability is high. This is 
backed by Apple's installed base growth, double digit YoY growth in services. However, the market 
would wait for visibility for its Apple Intelligence. Meanwhile, the market will need more visibility on the 
higher-high retail prices for the iPhone 18 series due to the rising cost over tariff and 2nm/memories. 
That said, we expect its earnings growth to be  high-teens to 20% YoY in FY26E-FY28E, from single 
digit YoY in FY22-FY24. Considering >30x one-year P/E, the stock doesn’t look particularly cheap.

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary:  We retain our near -term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid -term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3 -7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15 -year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US?  Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities  among all sc enarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer -term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fa llible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bond s (CoCos1) 
and US 10 -year go vernment bond ideas:  We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10 -year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:   Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share -price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monet isation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence.  We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight , while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral  and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent  technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long -term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro  area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro  area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While Fr ance’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro  
area bank equities  offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro  area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5 -3.0% q/q , led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased , despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs . Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro  area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited , supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in  China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as  a Core holding , as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in  AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weak ened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sect or contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation , leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk -reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10 -year yield fell  over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though.  Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y . Fed officials  have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

Significant share issuance has kept the market 
capitalisation fairly stable, while the MSCI China 
Index has declined c.20% over the past one year

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds , especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell -off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals . The sustained widening of the OAT -Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near -term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating rev iews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the  French presidential election . Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near -term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe -haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The Fr ance-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to  constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves  the CHF at a sizeable interest -
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90 , excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention . A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near -term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next  12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in Fr ench government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high  public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustment s are  weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s  10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premi a on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, e levated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi -year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid -2027, reducing USD support and driving  the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk  is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding  conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist
